### Post #13: 2025-01-25 18:22 — `$MU` `[News]`

**$MU moves to North Texas**

Micron is moving to Richardson's Innovation Quarter, which is a building in an area that focuses specifically on housing tech companies. This is in addition to space they already have in Allen. I know most of yall have owned this stock or traded it but might have forgotten what exactly it is that micron does. They specialize in storage and memory solutions for phones, cars, and companies. They make semiconductors and use cloud based storage solutions, which require servers that require a lot of power. So this is just news that could positively affect TX clean energy providers similar to the data center being built outside Abilene. Vistra and those portable nuclear energy providers seem to be really well after the Stargate announcement, especially NNE. Constellation is always up there but NNE has skyrocketed which is scary for a company that size that isn't profitable but they have the patents for energy sources that are huge for these data centers that will most certainly be used at least in partial to power these data centers across the US. They specialize in getting large amounts of power to obscure locations, which is a big deal for these data centers as its obvious they don't want to build them close to major cities. Those of you who have been to DFW and driven west for whatever reason know what I'm talking about. Abilene was a much larger deal in TX in the mid-1800s, and during the oil boom in the 80s as kind of the beginning of the Permian basin and west TX and Friday night lights. Just some info on increased energy consumption in TX. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/zIS/dollarmu-moves-to-north-texas*

### Post #24: 2025-02-26 02:37 — `$TEM, $HIMS` `[News]`

**Rough Seas**

So I gambled  $HIMS falling post earnings and in the morning it took Robinhood about 5 mins to correctly calculate the new premiums and I'm $3 in the money so I'm just hitting sell wanting to execute before buyers start scooping the dip throwing the pricing through even greater chaos. So robinhood conveniently executed the sale of my ITM 40P at 1.70 when it was worth 3.50 and ultimately $4.50. The bid ask had not properly gapped so when I sold I got bare minimum on a successful gamble that paid off but when you win these coin flips you want the big ass stuffed animal on the top shelf! Robinhood conveniently executes options for its benefit all the time as I'm sure everyone knows when trying to sell a volatile option that's up 500% and dropping $10 every sec and you sell and it doesn't execute. Sell again no execute again and again and finally execution with $200 out there in failed orders. Anyways I'm not crying just providing an experience to watch for. On the flip side I lowered my limit price to max I would pay for $TEM after it fell over night and that damn order posted but the ask price had not adjusted so it was immediately worth $3.50 and higher. I let this play out for a few minutes because I thought buyers were going to start buying the dip and it would continue to rise and I'm In at 1.10 and immediately selling for 3.50-4.50 then back down and couldn't sell fast enough. Just a heads up on crazy AH swings. The morning after its taking RObinhood a few minutes to calculate pricing correctly and you can take advantage of these anomalies. I longed $TEM pre ER and it fell so right before opening I dropped my limit price and that thing executed at my number unbelievable so the money a lost on the long gamble was made up on the long call in the morning which was followed by a long Put later in the day when heavy volume started coming in on puts. I'm not a day trader and this certainly does distract the shitvoutbof me while working but it can be done way more efficiently. This morning was a mad scramble. I was wanting to post so many plays I knew would benefit from the bearish volatility. You'll see $15 here and $10 there. Long winded way of saying there are some ways to take advantage of Robinhood trying to pocket your spread and execute your sales at the worst price and you can use that against them by scheduling a limit buy on a stock that plummeted with the strike price right below where it dropped and you'll have an option thatbin the money immediately and you'll have paid a low ask price for an option that's 3x-4x and just sell quick before the spread shrinks. Sorry Lotta words

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/Fx2/rough-seas*

### Post #117: 2025-07-11 07:40 — `$META, $MSFT, $AMD, $AMZN, $HOOD, $BABA` `[Dd]`

**$BABA & option premiums & Tech**

8/15 115C $2.79

The 8/15 110C is up 20%. I hate how robinhood calculates return on premium or the % change on each respective contract. They calculate it with the use of the previous days premium at closing. 

There is a ton of movement in share prices AH so obviously the following day at market open the premium is going to be recalculated. All the Greeks change, supply,  demand, volume. The most important changes are delta which measures the premiums correlation with the stock price. Gamma is a measurement of delta and theta is the time decay. 

If the option market is closed during the dark pool hours it's inaccurate to calculate the % change of that day based on the previous days   closing premium. It needs to be calculated based on the 1st ask price when the bid ask is calculated after the market opens because a lot of investors scalp so brokers should accommodate who makes up the majority of their customer base who actively manage their accounts. Anyways take the % change with a grain of salt. 

I bought $BABA 1st thing this morning and even raised my ask price prior to open and it didn't execute. RH will always raise  the bid ask based on your limit order. They want nothing more than for you to place a market order in which case they will give you the worst entry premium possible and you will automatically open (-). Same thing with exit and the bid price. Its their spread inside the spread. It is what it is and the only way I've seen to combat this is set your limit order in advance and all of a sudden you will see that option disappear. It's almost if you open the execution screen their algorithm goes into affect and you're constantly chasing the best entry and exit. 
Anyways I think this is a great price for stock that's rising based on all the important variables. 
Entries today sucked but buying the dips were great. There seems to be some tech exhaustion but it doesn't change reality or the TP's that are based on fundamentals, growth, liquidity, and most importantly demand. If you can buy the 8/15 expiration with a strike price that is within 5% of  the current stock price I think you will see 100% returns on the big names if you buy calls. That's a lot shit I wrote. The premiums are expensive I don't see how it's not a certainty that companies  like $META, $MSFT, $AMD, $AMZN  don't rise more than 5% by then. Then again Trump could happen. Outside of that all good.

$BABA  $HOOD $TESLA fuck it here... Have a great day everyone! Oh and we are taking profits on extrinsic value not actually exercising. Just want to be clear. Exp date is just risk mitigation. 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/SRl/dollarbaba-and-option-premiums*

### Post #209: 2025-08-23 04:28 — `$OPEN` `[Discuss]`

**$OPEN**

I brought this topic up with a member about a week ago and my thesis is playing out so kind of wanna see how far yall think this bus can make it before the wheels fall off. 

Over 50% of open is owned by retail so this thing can be be manipulated with any WSB dipshit throwing a treat on their forum floor. This can't be quantified outside of guaging how many shares are owned by impressionable meme crazed investors. This is typically the position funds are in as a group and we all know how they use that leverage. They can send out an email in the morning requesting everyone's trading desk sells x amount of shares until noon because we need a Lower entry point point. Collusion is easy if the top 20-30 funds usually make up the majority stake outside of the board and retail is left to react. Fundamentals suck. No CEO can correct a bad model and there are so many variables outside of the company's control. Bottom line the company isn't going anywhere even if rates cracked 3% again. 

That being said 50% ownership to retail cannot be ignored from an investing standpoint. This is obvious with the amount of short interest in a company that has a balance sheet made of toilet paper. So what do you do? This is like seeing a dude put down his mortgage on black as you're walking by the roulette table. Best believe I'm stopping watching where that ball lands. How much should you throw down just to enjoy the spin and cheer for black? That's kind of what I've been thinking about. I've been in and out but it's something that could last a bit longer even if they never reach profitability and there's no squeeze here. @sirjack has his hand on the pulse of retail being the OG YOLO retail legend. I don't know how you can chart this or forecast it when Financials mean shit and ER don't matter because they will always be bad but can be used as days where everyone decides to buy more. This is an interesting situation 🤔

**Today's positions Open & Closed**

This is everything and these positions are still running in both directions. I had to short some of  the ATHs. Gotta hit it from the front and the back and even sideways when the mood is right and you're feeling freaky get your straddle on. 

GLD expiring every other day can be extremely beneficial. Way less volatile then Russell's and what does gold normally do? Yea slowly but surely it appreciates. When this bullish cycle ends I'll switch to a protection fund basically compiled of energy, Healthcare, and defense stocks with govt contracts, gold, minerals, and few mining stocks and wal Mart. Throw in Costco. I left vistra off today. I usually have 70% TX companies and yall probably have a few too. Oracle, applied digital, asst. Devon is in OK but they make their living TX fracking the Permian. Oh and everyone's favorite grab a freakn gun. I've never taken a position on it but the store is guns r us. 

Microstrategy really hurt. Bought that spread over 40 days ago which is really not advisable because theta works for you with spreads just like selling calls and puts you gain value because as you approach expiration the value of the call loses value and the stock increasing offsets that along with gamma so it's almost like an amortization table you start paying more towards principle as your position amortizes and in the middle you are even with P&I and at full amortization you are paying 100% principle and 0 interest with a spread that call you sold is your interest and the call you bought is principle and 1 is worth 100% and the other 0 at exp so if you purchase long dated spreads that 2nd leg holds extrinsic value longer but I thought strategy would far surpass the 2nd leg and doing so you can max out the value way before expiration. I had 3 Tesla spreads going and I decided to go ahead and close them all because I'm making it a point to not sleep on maximum returns for that day. I do a quick opportunity cost calculation. What am i giving up by closing? With spreads it's easy because you're capped. Holding would have yielded an extra $200 if i heald to maturity and tesla finished above 440. It could have also fallen below 435 with its high IV.  Incorporate cost benefit analysis and it costs me nothing to close its just profit. It could cost me my principle if I don't. Upside a few hundred. How much have I profited so far? 50%. Downside is the total cost of the spread let's say $250. I've already made 50% so why would I risk profit plus principle for a few 100. Solution close. 

Crazy thing is RH left an open 440C. That's the leg I sold but somehow I own it. It gets way complicated, too much so for me to go into now cause y'all stopped reading once you saw cost benefit analysis but you can't open the opposite leg of a spread so I don't know how this happened but I own a 440C for tesla that I would never buy 3 DTE. So I was just gonna post positions when considering a volatile market, info from peeps on AH, and my own logic. I listen to and follow a few people on here and take their info seriously so I combine their knowledge with mine while sprinkling analyst info, news, and fundamentals, while using TPs or future value calculations from the analysts at 3 independent firms because I don't trust any analyst that works for a bank. ### Post #275: 2025-10-04 02:31 `[Discuss]`

**SPREADS Cont'd**

@work247  since spreads gain value as they near expiration the only thing that can really hurt you is a volatile stock that can just decide to drop 20 pts like appluvn or tesla depending on their moods. The other thing on far dated spreads is that the short leg will prevent your profitability because you're retaining more extrinsic value the further out you are so it's a situation where the leap might be too expensive and you might see others with naked calls gaining value as the stock rises but that short call is gonna act as a governor to your gamma which is your turbo  but if your feel strongly about a stock and don't want to take the risk of a short DTE contract you can still benefit from the gamma exposure an option provides you just buy a bunch of narrow spreads but also if the stock reaches the 2nd leg and you're still far from maturity it stifles your gains where as a wide spread with more time might be the better option. I've discussed these scenarios with my robots and it depends. I'm gonna take out multiple 2 pt spreads for onds expiring 1/26 or 3/26 maybe 11-13 or maybe just 11-12 because now the premiums are juiced but when buying spreads that doesn't matter.

### Post #311: 2025-10-13 03:58 — `$APLD` `[Dd]`

**$APLD**

Applied digital are data center construction ninjas. Their model is no different than residential or general commercial construction just with higher denominations. They are freakn deep in construction with the north dakota data centers, the one that the majority of the public associates with coreweave is conplete operational and generating revenue. I'm not sure most know that coreweave has a guaranteed lease for a portion of the data center. That's great and all but I think many thought coreweave was funding this buildout. Macquire asset mgmt is the investor financing the buildout and although the exact terms are not completely disclosed I'm sure it's a combination of interest, % of lease pmts, mgmt fees potentially, and equitable interest ie stock options for APLD. Like any other time when you secure financing outside of a bank that's a depository institution you're going to be looking at IO pmts with rate ranging from 8.99-13.99%. We in construction consider these hard money loans. 
Macquire asset mgmt has a large line of credit with their bank and they have wealthy investors that sit on their board and that is what they use to secure the LOC and fund these projects with companies like APLD. So they put applied digital on a draw schedule albiet the largest draw schedule in the history of construction.  The 1st is already complete and coreweave makes up 4 megawatts of 16 megawatt center and are making their lease pmts. APLD already has the remaining 12 megawatts leased out and the 2nd data center when complete is already at full occupancy. 
So in real estate if you don't have generational wealth or friends with millions of dollars companies use hard money. I've used millions of dollars of hard money ranging from 9.99-12.99% IO up to 75% ARV while funding 100% of the remodel or construction costs up to the 75% ARV. Some companies, 1 In particular,  have formulas like 70% of ARV - construction budget and if that number is above the acquisition cost you only have ro bring closing costs to the table. Trick is getting your comps up there so they are subtractiong your construction budget from a higher ARVs thus making it possible to get 100% financing essentially. Lenders like this charge 2 points on the front end and 1 pt upon  sale so its important to pay these loans off quick becauae of the interest rate. I know how quickly I can build and before that 1st pmt is due I try to get homes under contract prior to the 1st pmt or coupon request and the lender can collect 


at closing on the HUD. So $APLD is in position with their NOI to pay off Macquire early on data center 1 and 2 and they will never have to use them again. Once established their risk level falls and they can borrow what they want if they want but what will happen is they will fund the next data centers and they will manage and collect and then those data centers will be used as collateral to build more at rates around your residential mtg. 
So that's a long way of saying $APLD is on the fast track to being debt free and their EPS being positive. They are in a very good position to reach 1 bill NOI within 5 years. North dakota alone is gonna make $APLD evaluation rise around 25% by mid q1 of 26 conservatively. After companies don't need maquire or hard money it's game on. Most drown and are fire sold. Not this one. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/5dH/dollarapld*

### Post #328: 2025-10-16 06:09 — `$APLD` `[News]`

**Blackrock & NVDA data center partnership**

Long read

I guess NVDA said fuck waiting on APLD and others to build these things. We will just let our little adopted pets like coreweave partner with others while we secure financing to do it ourselves, and also remember coreweave didn't put up money they just guaranteed the lease for 15 years. The draws are coming from a fund I wrote about the other day and NVDA has plenty of cash but it's always better to use someone else's capital rather than your own especially when the only game your partner brings to the table is their liquidity. NVDA can pretty much go anywhere and ask them to do anything for them and they will. 
NVDA cannot fill these data centers fast enough with their chips so it's almost like they backed coreweave to stimulate the infrastructure portion of this AI boom while they worked behind the scenes to become vertically integrated. They still want $APLD  to build as many as fast as they can because no matter who occupies these centers or %s of centers NVDAs chips are going in them along with NVTS and others they have developed partnerships with. **Greed alive & well and on full display**

TLTR. So I had a closing scheduled for the 19th. The lender commits the cardinal sin and notifies all parties that they encountered a problem in final review. This was after they resolved the initial problem which was discovered last minute as well. This sale entered escrow after a 1 week option period 10/20 with a closing date of 11/19. This is a new construction purchase that started off as FHA and was flipped to conventional due to DAKA. I'll leave there but bullshit. 2 weeks to close conventional 

Typically if a buyer is going from 3.5% to 10% on a 480k purchase it's a deal killer but these buyers had 72k because the LO asked for 15% down. Over 80% LTV you have PMI private mortgage insurance. This house is on the edge of a flood plain so flood insurance is up to the lender and usually depends on LTV but it is discretionary unless the buyer provides the proper docs from FEMA that demonstrates clear they are outside the flood plain. In this case the structure is outside the flood plain but the lot itself enters the plain with portions of the boundary that is far away from the structure so the structure is not exposed whatsoever and the foundation is 4ft above BFE. 

I meet the buyers to go over key points of the foundation and piers going down to bedrock, warranties, and perks I provide,  and get to know them and their goals, if any, with the purchase of this home. This is an investment for the buyers as they are going to host airbnb guests. Myself, being a superhost, am certainly well versed and alrwady ran the numbers for this which is incredible. 3 hrs live on airbnb with pricing maxed at $250 person per night 8 guest max was fully booked for 3 weeks and I set the first availability 3 weeks out so I could cancel  immediately and turned off the auto booking where if they had 5 stars with min 5 bookings I didn't have to approve. 

When it flipped from FHA to conventional DTI changes (debt to income ratio). PITI and PMI and flood. The buyers are aware of flood insurance but some how itvgets to final review and the underwriter incredibly discovers it's In flood zone then includes the premium which takes their dti to 51% and it needs to be at 49%. The lender can lender paid mortgage insurance where the rate js higher and the lender is paid their insurance premium through yield thus eliminating the 150 PMI pmt. Buyer calls me crying saying he asking for 15k which I know will bring ltv under 80% avoiding PMI. This is the 3rd time he asked for more money. He didn't want to do lender paid MI because it would fuck with his yield spread when he fucked up multiple times. 

I tell them get it closed and I will build your entertainment vanity and rooftop pergola so that they can host and generate income immediately. This guy paints them into a corner where they are pot commited and have no choice because now 4800 escrow deposit is in jeopardy. The greed of some people never ceases to amaze. I've written enough but this asshole  would rather have me lower the price or the buyers give more than everything rather than give up a point because of their fuck up. This home is worth 650k. I think I've conceded enough. They even took out a loan for a portion of the DP. Almost free money SBA. Closes Wed. I told this idiot get docs to title Monday. Try tobfund the day before TG. Smh

### Post #501: 2026-03-11 16:17 — `$HIMS` `[Dd]`

**$HIMS**

Is kind of playing out like we actipated with the level of short interest and dealer hedging and retail jumping in as the they chase this plane down the tarmac. Retail will probably continue to jump in and it will consistently approach and pass these strikes with massive call exposure. 

So when dealers sell a bunch of calls they are short gamma and as the share price nears those strikes they have to purchase shares in order to stay delta neutral. 

Gamma is just a measure of delta and delta is the correlation between the premium and the stock price. The closer to 1 delta is the tighter the correlation is with the premium and share price. It will never reach 1 but if it did the premium would react dollar for dollar with the share price.

So as this plane reaches each of these strikes with high OI dealers are hedging by buying more shares in order to maintain their delta position which is usually like .4 but it's whatever it was when they sold the option.

 Anyways that's what causes this feedback loop and sustained large jumps if it there is huge OI all the way up the option chain. That's why I was like I got this shit staggered out to 2027. If your deep ITM the option premium is gonna lose its jet fuel and just kind of creep so you sell take your bag and ladder up obtaining more gamma exposure. I tried to make that the cliff notez version. Quick easy way to understand a sustained double digit growth when a stock was hammered into the ground and short interestvis damn near 50% and a positive catalyst, like the elimination of the short catalyst, sparks and ignites the trail of jet fuel.

### Post #503: 2026-03-13 16:52 `[Dd]`

**The complete AI bottleneck cheat code**

Cooling to substrates to the builders of data centers. There are more data centers in TX then strip clubs so that's a lot. Some of them are walking distance to the club if the peeps running the centers need to get some legs with their eggs or  just cool off. Speaking of cooling that's super important.  

If you just want 8 tickers go to the bottom. 

NVIDIA’s newer AI rack designs are pushing the industry harder toward liquid cooling. NVIDIA has said Blackwell rack-scale systems are liquid-cooled, and its GB300 NVL72 is a fully liquid-cooled rack design. NVIDIA also said full rack-scale Blackwell solutions can require up to 120 kW per rack, with a path to 1 MW IT racks in next-gen architectures. �
NVIDIA +2
That’s why this group matters more than old-school “HVAC stock goes up because data centers” thinking. After Jensen Huang’s January 2026 comments, the market specifically differentiated between companies tied to direct/liquid and precision cooling versus traditional water-chiller exposure. Reuters noted Vertiv and nVent were seen as better-positioned than some legacy HVAC names after those comments. �
Reuters
The public liquid-cooling names that actually matter
Tier 1: most direct / most relevant
Vertiv (VRT)
Best pure public name for the theme.
Why it matters:
Vertiv is heavily exposed to power + thermal management for AI data centers. �
Vertiv
In January 2026 it launched MegaMod HDX, integrating direct-to-chip liquid cooling with air-cooled architectures for high-density AI, from smaller pod deployments up to 10 MW configurations. �
Vertiv
In March 2026 it announced a partnership with Generate Capital to accelerate deployment of complete power and cooling infrastructure for data centers. �
Vertiv
It also bought PurgeRite in late 2025 to strengthen its liquid-cooling services portfolio. �
Reuters
My take: if you want one flagship public liquid-cooling name, it’s VRT.
Modine (MOD)
This is the one that fits your “smaller, still torquey, real bottleneck” style better.
Why it matters:
Modine announced $180 million of data-center cooling orders in February 2025 from a new AI infrastructure customer. �
Modine Manufacturing Company
It then committed $100 million to expand North American data-center cooling capacity in July 2025. �
Modine Manufacturing Company
It opened a new Wisconsin facility in November 2025 as part of that expansion. �
Modine Manufacturing Company
In 2026 it launched TurboChill 3+MW, aimed at the water-temperature and efficiency demands of modern AI data centers. �
Modine Manufacturing Company
My take: MOD is the cleaner “next rung down from Vertiv” trade. Less crowded, more torque.
nVent (NVT)
A quieter name that has gotten more interesting.
Why it matters:
nVent unveiled a new liquid-cooling and high-density power portfolio in late 2025, including a CDU based on Google OCP specifications. �
nVent Investors +1
It expanded manufacturing in Minnesota specifically to meet rising liquid-cooling demand. �
nVent Investors
Reuters flagged nVent as one of the names better aligned with the move toward liquid and precision cooling after Nvidia’s CES comments. �
Reuters
My take: NVT is a sneaky picks-and-shovels name because it hits both cooling and electrical infrastructure.
Tier 2: bigger/safer but less “torque”
Eaton (ETN)
Why it matters:
Eaton is a major data-center power player already.
In November 2025, Reuters reported Eaton agreed to buy Boyd’s thermal business for $9.5 billion, giving it liquid-cooling capability at scale; that business was expected to generate about $1.7 billion in 2026 revenue, mostly from data centers. �
Reuters
NVIDIA has also named Eaton among partners in its next-gen 800 VDC ecosystem for AI factories. �
NVIDIA Developer
My take: ETN is less of a pure cooling trade and more of a power + thermal platform trade. Safer, less explosive.
Carrier (CARR), Trane (TT), Johnson Controls (JCI)
These are real cooling companies, but for this specific AI liquid-cooling theme they’re not my favorites.
Why:
Reuters reported these names sold off after Nvidia’s January 2026 comments because the market worried about reduced need for traditional water chillers in next-gen AI systems. �
Reuters
My take: they’re not useless, just not the cleanest expression of the AI liquid-cooling bottleneck.
The quickest ranking
Best pure AI liquid-cooling exposure
VRT
MOD
NVT
Bigger, steadier compounders
ETN
VRT
NVT
Highest torque
MOD
NVT
VRT
(VRT is bigger, but the market still trades it like a core AI infra winner.)
How I’d think about it with your style
You usually like:
real bottleneck
public name
not completely obvious
can still move
That points me to:
Best fit for you
MOD
NVT
VRT
If you want the cleanest basket
VRT = category leader
MOD = smaller torque
NVT = sneaky hybrid winner
The whole AI infrastructure group I’d actually keep on one watchlist

VST, ETN, NVT, PWR

### Post #529: 2026-04-17 03:22 — `$CRDO, $IPGP` `[Dd]`

**OSAT Metrology Manufacturing Testing & Optics(Lasers) Watchlists AI Ecosystem**

So I seperate each sector of the market into its own watchlist and simply rank the companies from highest to lowest on a daily basis which your broker will do for you. 

This enables you to see where big money is being invested and what is least exposed. This is is never random and there is always and obvious pattern. There are the green teens on top that most likely are parabolic on normal days where the president doesn't say shit or I guess that would be an abnormal day. 

There is the middle or accumulation section of the sector and there is the red portion which is the least exposed and was likely leading the sector the previous day or 2 days prior.  

You never enter anything at the top cause you already missed the run and youbwill overpay and notbhave any gamma left. You can enter the middle as long as you follow your triggers and stay disciplined. Share price at or above vwap. Don't be hugging the top of the BB. Macd needs to curl up slightly. Rsi shouldn't be above senior living care. ATR Should be shrinking because the bands are tightening which is an indicator of coiling and pressure building for a push that will either confirm continuation or a breakout after catching support and buyers stepping in. 

Look for bottom wicks applying upward pressure after catching the share price in a decline and pushing 🫸 upwards. All funds do is rotate capital. They get a sector moving up and wait for retail to notice and after retail jumps in adding some kerosene to a fire that they started they start easing out reallocating capital gains to the names below the top of the watchlist. It's just rinse and repeat once you identify the pattern. It's not day trading but it's actively managing your book using options to generate higher returns and using returns to load up on the handful of positions you have High Long term conviction in. 

I wrote too much but I was just trying to tell my guy @DocHollywood that I wasn't chasing $CRDO with this micro book mgmt. I was actually entering on a dip after a big run. The fact that doc like $CRDO is like a positive top tier analyst review for me. I combine watchlists with TA from the usual suspects (kingdom)and insane analysis by @mphinance Got a bunch of useful tools and just use them the right way. That sounded terrible. Software Salesforce and APP are solid. I wrote too much. ✌️ $IPGP IS THE other company I picked up today and If you look at AH pricing it's already starting to absorb capital after the dip.

### Post #533: 2026-04-21 07:32 — `$AAOI` `[Dd]`

**How to treat these pix & shovels Metrology and optics plays that run like a Yangwang U9X.**

Since the option tree is priced outrageously on the ones that move I prefer to use call debit spreads. If you have a high risk tolerance and don't give a shit about mitigating risk and just want to run wide open by all means buy naked calls ATM or slightly OTM with 14+ DTE. 

The reason the premiums are so high is because market makers are gonna tax that ass because they are unsure how much they will have to hedge to stay delta neutral as these share prices near strikes on the way up the chain. Obviously the strikes with the highest OI will require the largest amount of hedging which creates a vacuum  or magnet sucking that share price up as they purchase shares to stay delta neutral. This caps your gains but if your comfortable with exiting at 25%+ ROI consistently then you will be fine. 

This is an alternative to selling CC's because some of these share prices are becoming quite expensive and picking up 100 shares of all the stocks that run like Bugatti's can become quite expensive although the high IV does justify insane premium revenue but good luck picking a strike that doesn't get your shares called away. 

Honestly it wouldn't be a bad idea running a wheel on $AAOI . Just ask @DocHollywood. This particular stock runs  so hard and fast for so long sometimes that your safest strikes become liabilities and what are u gonna gonna do? BTC because you don't wanna lose your shares. This is where understanding the rotation allocation comes in handy. Look at where $AAOI sits right now compared to its teen green days just last week. You can sell a csp at a strike which was it's previous high and now is its new support and gain back the shares and sell CCs using strikes at its new resistance or you can just buy call debit spreads when it recycles to the bottom of the sector and buy put debit spreads when money is rotating out and its on top. Plenty of options once you identify the rotation 

Here are very conservative returns using this simple strategy. You can hang on longer but I like to close and keep up the rotation. I'm more conservative and cannot be completely consumed with trading all day so I enter safe positions with lower risk and reward where I don't have to check my phone all day.  Aight cul8rg8rs.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/bmCI/how-to-treat-these-pix-and-sho*

Ultra-pure
Structurally perfect (no dislocations/defects)
Uniform across the wafer

The entire wafer is trash
Downstream chip yields collapse

### Post #583: 2026-05-16 06:00 — `$NKE` `[Dd]`

**$NKE   77.5    1/15/27**

So  I was adding staggered calls of Nike leaps thanks to @tron mentioning creative new lines. I just wear different colors of AJ1s and air max 90s, Luka 1 2 3 4, and dunks to match different tshirts. I am confident they will turn it around although I don't give a fuck about whatever new designs they have to come out with to do so. 

77.5 1/15/27 call has OI of 26,680. That's an obscure strike for that level of open interest. It's not retail long calls so rule that out. It's not someone selling a bunch of CCs that long dated and it's not MM selling them to balance their books but it is a MM that had to fill that order. 

Who would order that? That is institutional market manipulation in the sense someone is looking 5 moves ahead like Luka can maneuver a defense to do what he wants to either get his shot or free a cutter or hit his roller. That strike is so obscure that to place that order would force 1 mm to fill it, probably citadel. Now you force 1 entity to hedge as $NKE rises as a pose to several. Funds know MM hedge points and the level of delta they need to keep so this now gives the fund the knowledge of what the share price will do once it hits certain thresholds. As the share price rises and MM are short gamma they have to buy more to stay delta neutral which is why strikes with high OI become vacuums. 

This is different. Every 5 pts or so that MM that's short those calls is going to have to pick up shares. There are other ways to maintain neutrality but this is a slick move to long that weird ass strike and it's bullish. It's done with the conviction Nike will hit that strike and the buyer of that call just sped up the process by isolating 1 market maker which gives them the ability to buy more shares when they know that MM is at their threshold and they will have to buy more shares to maintain their delta neutrality. 

I've never seen this move before but I've never seen someone as slow as Luka control games and fuck up teams that are going to dominate for 10 years like the thunder and spurs. Ultimately buy Nike leaps or shares or whatever the fuck you want as long as it's bullish.

Nick, this is where your “who owns the oil fields?” framework gets interesting. I’d separate data collectors from data owners with monetizable moats. A lot of companies collect data; far fewer own data that others can't easily recreate.
Pipestack-style ranking: upside + institutional adoption potential
Tier 1 — Adult table / likely institutional accumulation
1. duolingo.com⁠� Why:
Massive behavioral dataset
Reinforcement learning loops
Speech + progression + retention data
AI-native already
Upside thesis: Not just "language learning." It's human learning behavior at scale.
Risk: Valuation can get stretched.
Fund ownership feel:
⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐
2. chegg.com⁠� Why:
Huge education/Q&A corpus
Structured knowledge
Student interaction patterns
Upside: Can get violent if the market narrative shifts from:
"AI killed them"
to:
"AI needs their data."
Risk: Execution.
Fund ownership feel:
⭐⭐⭐
3. veeva.com⁠� Why:
Pharma workflows
Clinical data
Drug lifecycle data
Medical data is expensive and difficult to recreate.
Fund ownership feel:
⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐
4. palantir.com⁠� Why:
Organizes fragmented datasets
Government + enterprise relationships
Turns messy information into usable intelligence
Not a collector in the pure sense:
more refinery than oil field
Fund ownership feel:
⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐
5. crowdstrike.com⁠� Why:
Cyber telemetry
Threat intelligence
Real-time behavioral data
Cyber data compounds over time.
Fund ownership feel:
⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐
Tier 2 — Narrative runners / could get heat
6. onemednet.com⁠�
Interesting because:
clean medical imaging
hard-to-replicate datasets
AI training potential
But:
Small cap + weaker structure.
This is more:
potential future adult table candidate
than adult table today.
Where I think your thinking is strongest
You said:
"Narrative creates a run → momentum catches → retail FOMO"
That absolutely happens.
But institutions usually don't say:
"Let's buy the hot AI data stock."
They say:
"Which datasets become more valuable every year?"
That's why slow accumulation often happens in:
healthcare
cybersecurity
enterprise workflow
learning behavior
My ranking if I combine:
moat + upside + likelihood funds keep adding

Veeva
Chegg

😂 I found it. You absolutely unlocked that whole sector-personality bit.
I said:
"The laser kids are throwing chairs... OUST is doing backflips... HIMX is screaming in Mandarin volatility... and AMAT is sitting there like: 'We literally supply the machinery that prints the future. Calm down.'"
And the reason it landed was because we were talking about behavior, not the companies themselves:
HIMX
Gap up 6–8%
Flush 5%
Reclaim VWAP
Lose VWAP
Rip again
Whole chart looked caffeinated
Meanwhile AMAT/KLAC were:
"We're still collecting purchase orders and cash flow. Carry on."
The funny part is there was actually a serious point under the joke:
Smaller thematic names = thinner liquidity + momentum money + faster dealer hedging + sharper rotations
AMAT/KLAC = institutions can park serious size there without turning the chart into a roller coaster
So the board was acting like:
HIMX: "EVERYBODY GET IN HERE WE'RE GOING TO THE MOON"
AMAT: "Please submit your capex request in an orderly fashion." 😆The read we built was:
AMAT being green or stable while optics and memory were weak was sneaky bullish, because funds weren't abandoning AI capex — they were parking money in core infrastructure.
The logic behind calling it "the adult":
Funds can park size there
Less random high-beta behavior
Semi equipment = picks-and-shovels
Doesn't need euphoric retail momentum
Tends to ladder higher instead of launching and faceplanting
You also had that spread around then:
430/435 CDS
Stock around 436
Spread moved roughly +47% while AMAT itself wasn't doing anything crazy
And that was basically the lesson:
The board was screaming, throwing chairs, and AMAT was sitting in the corner reading quarterly capex reports.

vs

some optics names

### Post #615: 2026-05-21 02:56 — `$ANET, $ALAB` `[Dd]`

**$ALAB & $ANET cliff note version. Like Shakespeare for peeps that hate Shakespeare**

@geo21208 
Nick, using your PIPESTACK style, I’d strip it down to "who moves the data and who directs the traffic."
ALAB (Astera Labs)
Astera Labs
Simple version:
ALAB builds the traffic cops and smart intersections inside AI servers.
Imagine an AI data center like a city:
GPUs = factories making products
Memory = warehouses
Data = trucks
ALAB = intelligent highways/interchanges controlling traffic
Without ALAB-type products:
trucks pile up
GPUs sit waiting
expensive AI hardware gets idle
GPUs hate waiting.
If you buy a Ferrari and it sits at a red light all day, you wasted your money.
ALAB reduces those red lights.
ANET (Arista Networks)
Arista Networks
Simple version:
ANET builds the roads between cities.
ALAB handles movement inside the AI machine/server rack.
ANET handles movement between racks, buildings, and entire data centers.
If OpenAI has:
50,000 GPUs
multiple data halls
multiple clusters
ANET keeps all of those talking to each other.
Why both are essential
Think of a giant home development since that's your world:
You build:
houses = GPUs
streets inside subdivision = ALAB
highways connecting subdivisions = ANET
You can build gorgeous houses all day...
But if:
streets are jammed → houses become inefficient
highways are jammed → nobody gets there
You need both.
Why ALAB can run first
Because ALAB sits closer to the immediate bottleneck.
Funds think:
"We just added 100,000 GPUs. How do we stop those GPUs from sitting idle?"
Answer:
connectivity
PCIe
memory expansion
retimers
scale-up infrastructure
That's ALAB territory.
ALAB also:
smaller market cap
thinner liquidity
higher beta
So when funds rotate in:
$100M entering ALAB moves it more violently.
Why ANET can theoretically run second
Once money says:
"Okay, the GPU cluster itself is getting built..."
Next question becomes:
"How are all these clusters connected?"
Then money rotates toward:
networking
switches
optical infrastructure
That's where:
ANET
CRDO
LITE
CIEN
COHR
start getting attention.
Very similar to how you described your board:
Core AI narrative starts
Immediate bottlenecks move first (ALAB)
Infrastructure underneath moves (ANET)
Secondary suppliers rotate afterward
If I compress it into one line:
ALAB = roads inside the neighborhood
ANET = interstate highways connecting cities
No traffic flow = very expensive GPUs sitting around doing nothing.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/boNy/dollaralab-and-dollaranet-clif*

 Yeah, now that's what it looks like.
And that's a very different statement than:
"CRDO is broken."
Looking at your screenshots, especially the 5-minute after-hours chart:
Flush from ~$236 to ~$188.
Immediate buyers step in.
Bounce back toward ~$200.
Selling pressure starts decreasing.
Candles get smaller.
Volume dries up.
MACD starts recovering from an extreme negative reading.
RSI stabilizes instead of continuing lower.
That's classic post-event price discovery.
The market had to figure out where the real buyers were.
Apparently they showed up around $188-$195.
What I'd be watching tomorrow:
Bullish
Holds above $195.
Reclaims $205.
Starts building a base between $195-$210.
Volume contracts while price stabilizes.
That would support your consolidation thesis.
Bearish
Opens weak.
Fails at $205-$210.
Rolls back under $195.
Starts making lower lows below the after-hours low.
Then institutions may still be unwinding.
What stands out to me is that the stock did not stay at the low.
If institutions truly wanted out at any price, it doesn't bounce 10+ points off the lows that quickly.
The buyers showed up.
The question is whether those buyers are:
Short-covering.
Dip buyers.
Institutions accumulating.
Tomorrow will tell us which one.
And from your PipeStack perspective, this is still the same company that sits in one of the most important bottlenecks on the board: connectivity.
The fundamental question tomorrow isn't "is connectivity important?"
It's:
"Was the after-hours move an overreaction relative to the long-term story?"
The chart right now says the market is trying to answer exactly that question.
The one thing I wouldn't call it yet is a recovery. A recovery reclaims levels.
What I see right now is stabilization after a flush. That's the first step before a recovery can even begin.

-What do you know the catalysts were that instigated this sell off and why is it that we truly feel this was unexpected unless youre a perma bear and think every stock sucks and you kind of find satisfaction from  watching others go throuh heartbreak?

Nick, I don't think this was expected by most of the bulls.
If it were expected, CRDO wouldn't have been making fresh highs into the print.
What I think happened is a combination of three things:

1. The stock got ahead of itself
This thing ran from roughly $150 to $250 in a very short period.
Not because the business is bad—because the business is phenomenal.
The problem is that when a stock runs that hard, the earnings bar becomes:
"Blow away numbers, blow away guidance, blow away future expectations."
Instead they delivered:
"Excellent."
The market had already priced in "superhuman." � I had gone over this prior to holding my position into and past ER
Investors.com +1
2. Institutions focused on margins and future profitability
Not because margins are weak.
They're ridiculously strong.
But management basically signaled margins are stabilizing around the high-60s rather than continuing to expand. When you're trading at one of the richest multiples in semis, institutions scrutinize that stuff. �
Investors.com
3. Positioning was crowded
This is the one that matches your observation.
The reaction was too violent for:
"Oh no, the business is deteriorating."
Nothing in the report says that.
Revenue exploded. EPS exploded. Guidance beat.
The move looks more like a trade unwind than a fundamental thesis collapse. �
Investors.com +1
What I find interesting is this:
CRDO has actually done versions of this before.
Beat. Raise. Sell off.
Then recover later once people realize the story didn't change. There are multiple references this year to post-earnings "margin scares" and valuation resets despite strong operating results. �
Trefis
Now let's talk like PipeStack guys for a second.
What changed tonight about:
AI clusters?
Data center buildouts?
Scale-out networking?
Active electrical cables?
Optical DSPs?
Power efficiency?
Connectivity bottlenecks?
Nothing.
The connectivity layer didn't suddenly become less important at 4:01 PM.
The reason I wouldn't call this a fundamental break is because the reason you own Credo still exists.
The market is debating valuation.
You're debating infrastructure demand.
Those are not the same debate.
What I'm watching now is exactly what you're watching:
If buyers continue defending that $190-$200 area and it starts building a base, then the market may be telling us:
"We hated the valuation, not the company."

-We discussed whether or not it was priced in, an earnings beat was priced in prior to earnings, when the pre-earnings valuation represented a superior call and incredible guidance, which happens a lot. And if it's already priced in, it can either go sideways afterwards or fall tremendously to the previously accounted valuation. In this case, I don't think Credo was overvalued at all, and it wasn't priced in, and we agreed on that.
That's a very different message than:
"We hate the company."
And from everything I've seen so far, this looks much closer to the first one than the second. �

And this is where I'd separate two things:
What we believed before earnings
versus
What the market did after earnings.
Because those aren't always the same.
I actually remember our discussions pretty well. The argument wasn't:
"CRDO will beat earnings."
That part was almost assumed.
The argument was:
"Is the connectivity layer still underappreciated relative to the importance it plays in AI infrastructure?"
And on that question, I was largely in agreement with you.
You weren't buying CRDO because you thought they'd earn $1.03 instead of $1.00.
You were buying CRDO because you viewed it as one of the core bottlenecks in the PipeStack ecosystem.
Where I think you're right is this:
If the market had truly priced in everything perfectly, we'd likely see:
Beat
Raise
Flat stock
or
Small move
Instead we got a violent repricing.
That suggests something else happened.
The thing I keep coming back to is positioning.
Not valuation.
Positioning.
Those are different.
A stock can be fairly valued and still have too many people leaning the same way ahead of earnings.
When that happens:
Great report.
Some funds take profit.
Stops trigger.
Options dealers hedge.
Momentum funds leave.
And suddenly you're down 15-20%.
What makes me hesitate to call it "priced in" is exactly what you're pointing out.
The secular story didn't change.
Connectivity didn't become less important.
Hyperscalers didn't stop building.
AI clusters didn't stop scaling.
Data center networking didn't become optional.
So if the reason for owning CRDO was:
"The world needs more high-speed connectivity."
Nothing from the report disproved that.
The question that matters now is the one you and I won't know tonight:
Did institutions sell because they think future growth is slowing?
or
Did institutions sell because they had enormous gains and needed liquidity?
Those are very different.
The first one is bearish.
The second one is often temporary.
And honestly, if I look at this through your framework rather than a generic Wall Street framework, the thing I'd be paying attention to over the next few sessions isn't the earnings beat or miss.
It's whether money stays in the connectivity group.
Because if:
ANET stays strong
Connectivity names stabilize
Optics remain strong
Infrastructure remains strong
then CRDO may have simply become the source of funds for a day.
If the entire connectivity thesis starts unwinding, that's a different conversation.
I think where we agree is that tonight's reaction doesn't appear to match a company whose fundamental thesis just got destroyed. That's why you're questioning it. If they had missed, guided down, and talked about weakening demand, there'd be no mystery. The mystery is that the business still sounds strong, yet the stock got hit like something fundamentally broke. That's why I think the next few sessions are going to be more informative than the earnings headline itself.

I think you're making an important distinction that gets lost when people say "priced in."
There are really two different questions:
Question 1
Was the stock price already assuming a beat and raise?
That's what you're talking about.
And your argument was:
CRDO was not acting like a stock that had already priced in a monster quarter.
Compared to a lot of AI names, that's true.
Before earnings it wasn't making fresh all-time highs every week.
It wasn't doing what AAOI did today.
It wasn't doing what PLTR has done at times.
It had already experienced significant pullbacks and periods of sideways action.
That's why you didn't view it as a classic "sell the news" setup.
Question 2
Was the company overvalued?
That's a completely separate question.
And frankly, that's where reasonable people can disagree.
Your thesis has never been:
"CRDO should trade at 20x earnings because it's a semiconductor company."
Your thesis is:
"CRDO occupies a bottleneck position in one of the most important layers of AI infrastructure."
That's why you've consistently grouped it with ANET rather than some random semiconductor.
Because the question isn't:
How many chips did they sell?
It's:
How important is the connectivity layer as clusters scale?
Where I think your frustration comes from is this:
If:
Revenue beats
EPS beats
Guidance beats
and
The secular story remains intact
then the market reaction feels disconnected from the operating results.
That's a legitimate observation.
The thing I'd be careful about is immediately concluding that because the business is worth 30% more, the market will agree on your timeline.
You've seen this in real estate.
A house can be worth $600k.
That doesn't mean the next buyer tomorrow pays $600k.
Sometimes the market gets there later.
Sometimes it overshoots.
Sometimes it undershoots.
Stocks do the same thing.
What I think is more interesting is your original point:
CRDO wasn't behaving like a stock that had already fully discounted an earnings blowout.
That's why this reaction is surprising.
If the stock had run from $150 to $350 in three months, I'd say:
Yeah, expectations got absurd.
But that's not the chart we were looking at.
The chart we were looking at was a stock that had already spent time consolidating after earlier gains.
That's why I don't think "it was all priced in" fully explains tonight's move.
Personally, I think the more likely explanation is:
Strong earnings.
Strong guidance.
A heavily crowded institutional trade.
Some concern around future margin trajectory/customer concentration.
A lot of fast money deciding to lock gains simultaneously.
That can create a move that's much larger than the actual change in the business.
And that's the part I'd focus on over the next week:
Does the stock continue falling after institutions have had time to digest the report?
If the answer is yes, the market may be reassessing the future.
If the answer is no, and buyers start defending the $190-$200 area, then tonight may end up looking more like a positioning flush than a fundamental reassessment.
That's the piece we don't know yet. But I agree with you that the earnings report itself doesn't look like the kind of report that would normally justify the reaction if you only looked at the operating results.

### Post #686: 2026-06-09 08:14 — `$AMKR, $AEHR, $ACMR` `[Dd]`

**$AEHR**

They make the equipment that companies like $AMKR & $ACMR use to test semiconductor equipment. They just make the equipment. 

Think of the stack:
AEHR
Sells the burn-in and reliability testing equipment.
⬇
AMKR / ASE / SPIL
Use that equipment to perform packaging and testing services for customers.
⬇
NVDA / AVGO / MRVL / Automotive OEMs
Pay the OSATs to package and test their chips.
It's similar to construction.
You buy a skid steer from CAT.
Your concrete crew uses the skid steer.
The homeowner pays for the finished driveway.
CAT isn't pouring concrete.
The concrete contractor is.
But CAT still gets paid because the contractor needs the machine.
That's why AEHR actually sits one layer upstream from the OSATs.
Your ecosystem might look like:
Equipment
AMAT
KLAC
ONTO
CAMT
AEHR
⬇
OSAT / Packaging
AMKR
ASE
SPIL
⬇
Chip Designers
NVDA
AVGO
MRVL
AMD
⬇
End Markets
AI
Data Centers
Optics
Autonomous
Power
The reason I like finding names like AEHR is because they sometimes give you exposure to multiple end markets at once.
If AMKR wins, AEHR can win.
If a photonics company builds more capacity, AEHR can win.
If silicon carbide ramps, AEHR can win.
They're selling the shovel rather than digging for gold themselves.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/Dallaslongcall/bqt2/dollaraehr*

The thing I think Wall Street is missing is exactly what created the mosaic.
A rocket launch isn't the product.
The rocket is the delivery truck.
The value is:
What gets built before launch.
What gets deployed after launch.
What generates recurring revenue once it's in orbit.
That's why I think names like RDW, MNTS, ASTS, and IPX may actually have more room for surprise than the obvious "SpaceX sympathy trade" names. The crowd immediately buys RKLB. The bigger opportunity is often one or two layers deeper in the supply chain, just like AI

### Post #795: 2026-08-20 14:20 `[Dd]`

**Warning signs and prevention measures and post liquidation action items**

Monday’s semiconductor strength actually hid the danger. The real setup was:
Semiconductors were already the market’s most crowded trade.
Oil and long-term yields were rising sharply.
The broader market was weakening while semiconductors kept climbing.
Thin August liquidity allowed basket/program selling to cascade through every connected AI-infrastructure group.
Once the first major levels broke, funds sold the liquid winners, ETFs transmitted it across the ecosystem, and stop-loss/dealer/quant activity accelerated it. That is why AXTI, CAMT, CRDO, AMKR, optics, memory and equipment all collapsed together. Low summer liquidity amplified the move.
What we should have done
Closing everything Monday would have worked, but that is hindsight. The repeatable process would have been:
Monday afternoon: cut 50–70% of all 3–7 DTE semiconductor exposure, because semiconductors were climbing against worsening oil, yields and breadth.
Retain only the strongest positions: CDNS, ANET and the lower ALAB spread.
Tuesday premarket: Nasdaq futures were materially underperforming S&P futures and semiconductors were gapping down. That triggered an exit of the remainder on the first bounce.
Our mistake was recognizing AXTI’s individual volatility but not recognizing that eight different AI/semi positions had become one enormous correlated position.
Prevention system going forward
Condition
Action
Three prior-day warnings
Cut 50–70% of short-dated ecosystem exposure
Two premarket warnings
No new calls; sell/hedge remaining exposure on first failed bounce
Liquidation confirmed after open
No averaging; exit weak positions into any VWAP bounce
Re-entry
SOXX reclaims VWAP, at least half the mosaic turns green, and yields/oil stop climbing
The warnings are:
Strong semiconductor day while SPY, QQQ or equal-weight breadth deteriorates.
Oil and the 10/30-year yields rising simultaneously.
Great earnings being sold—AMAT’s Friday reaction was important.
SOXX/SMH extended approximately 20% over several weeks.
Nasdaq futures underperforming S&P futures by 0.5%+.
SOXX/SMH opening 1.5%+ lower.
Seventy percent or more of our ecosystem red simultaneously.
I updated our saved overlay with this correlation rule.
Rest-of-week outlook
My base case is a partial reflex recovery 

Wednesday or Thursday, but not an automatic full V-shaped recovery. The strongest names should recover first; the severely damaged smaller names may bounce without getting anywhere near our strikes.
Wednesday is the pivotal day:
20-year Treasury auction at 1:00 p.m. ET. Treasury schedule⁠�
FOMC minutes at 2:00 p.m. ET. Federal Reserve calendar⁠�
A solid auction and less-hawkish minutes could pull yields down and ignite a semiconductor squeeze. A poor auction or hawkish minutes could produce another leg lower. Friday is monthly options expiration, meaning considerable pinning and violent strike-related movement.
The recovery signals are:
SOXX above approximately 537 and holding.
QQQ above approximately 723.
30-year yield falling below approximately 5.25%.
Oil backing away from $90+.
ALAB reclaiming $300–303 and holding.
Another liquidation leg becomes likely if SOXX loses approximately 523, QQQ loses approximately 716, or the 30-year yield pushes through approximately 5.35%.
Open-position ranking
Based on prices around 3:00 p.m. ET:
Position
Assessment
CDNS 325/330, 8/28
Best overall. Only needs roughly 2% to reach the long strike and has time.
ALAB 295/300, 8/21
Best Friday position. $300 is the pivot; a modest recovery can max it.
LRCX 330/335, 8/21
Viable. Needs approximately 2% to reach $330.
ANET 200/205, 8/21
Relative strength, but needs approximately 3% just to reach $200.
NBIS 260/265, 8/21
High risk. Needs approximately 5% to enter the spread. Use a strong bounce to recover capital.

ALAB 325/330, 8/21
Long shot. An ordinary rebound will not save it.
CAMT 175/180, 8/21
Effectively a lottery ticket after today’s damage. Do not add.
NBIS 280/290, 8/28
Speculative but still has time and Nvidia earnings on August 26. Nvidia IR⁠�

There will probably be a recovery. The problem is that recovery next week does nothing for the 8/21 spreads. We need to use the first legitimate sector bounce to separate the viable positions from those merely getting a temporary options reprice.

And yes—defense belongs in our hedge sleeve. RTX, LMT and NOC are legitimate geopolitical/oil-shock hedges. Software is useful specifically against semiconductor rotation, as today demonstrated, but it is not a dependable hedge against a broad rising-yield liquidation because software is also duration-sensitive. Cash remains the cleanest hedge.

The ideal structure going forward is:
AI/semi mosaic as the primary trade.

Defense exposure established on quiet or red days—not chased after a 3% geopolitical pop.

Select software when its board begins outperforming.

Cash or a small tactical SMH/SOXX put spread when our yellow warnings activate.

You’re right. I was treating the quoted market percentage as the true probability, which completely missed your edge. An 84¢ price is the market’s price—not necessarily the player’s real probability. That’s the same mistake as treating a stock’s price as its intrinsic value.
I ran the exact test you proposed using the ATP match database: men’s Grand Slam first rounds from 2016–2025, classified by ATP ranking at tournament entry. �
GitHub
Player tier
First-round record
Win rate
Five all winning
World No. 1
36–0
100%
100%
Top 2
69–1
98.6%
93.1%
Top 3
104–3
97.2%
86.7%
Top 5
161–13
92.5%
67.8%
Top 10
321–36
89.9%
58.8%
But your strategy isn’t blindly selecting every top-10 player. The player-by-player results prove your distinction:
Djokovic: 33–0
Federer: 14–0
Alcaraz: 14–0
Sinner: 12–0
Nadal: 23–1
That elite, proven-major group went 96–1—98.97%. Five players with that empirical profile all winning works out to approximately 95%, not the generic 59% calculation I gave you.
Meanwhile, the players you would not automatically call locks performed very differently:
Tsitsipas: 16–4
Rublev: 12–3
Thiem: 15–4
Dimitrov: 6–3
That is exactly your point. “Top 10” is not the complete filter. We are identifying the Djokovic/Sinner/Alcaraz class: proven major performers, favorable first-round opponent, surface advantage, motivation and best-of-five protection. A market may sell those contracts at 80–90¢ even though the selected player’s true probability is closer to 95–99%.
At the US Open specifically, all top-10 entrants went 79–7, or 91.9%, during that decade. But the genuinely elite major anchors were essentially perfect. The famous-name selections matter, and so does eliminating fragile, erratic or surface-mismatched members of the top 10.
So yes: a carefully selected five-match first-round major combination can legitimately be much safer than its quoted 2× payout implies. Djokovic at 84¢ looked like Christmas because, absent the health information, his empirical profile said it was. The strategy wasn’t the problem. The publicly available Cincinnati medical flag was the problem—and failing to incorporate it caused us to dramatically overestimate his true probability that night. 

The top players, the elite players do not lose in the first round of grand slams, and they typically do not lose until they start playing each other. So your odds of winning these 5 match, 4 match parlays, when you're dealing with the top players are much greater than your stamdard odds of winning a parlay under normal circumstances outside of Grand Slam play, and we did a statistical analysis and found it to be true. 

The odds of this happening again, I slimmed a nun, and with Sinner out of the tournament, the the other top 8 seeds. I think are very safe plays. If you want to bet on the u s open not to win it all but parlay, or straight up

several high-quality infrastructure and data center names have already printed the signature institutional "V-shaped" dip-reversal candle intraday, but their percentage gains are still parked near the middle or lower tier of the board (+0.6% to +1.3%).
​These are the laggards showing early signs of institutional accumulation before catching up to the runaway leaders (like VRT, TSSI, and POWL).
​Prime Setup Candidates (Institutional Reversal Lagging Price)

​1. Modine Manufacturing (MOD) — +0.68%
​The Role: Pure-play competitor and peer to Vertiv in data center thermal management and liquid cooling systems.

​Why the Edge: If institutional money is rotating wholesale into AI cooling and thermal engineering today, MOD is fundamentally tied to the same trade and has significant room to play catch-up to the rest of the cooling basket.

​2. CoreWeave (CRWV) & Nebius Group (NBIS) — +0.91% to +1.16%
​The Role: Direct AI "Neocloud" and GPU infrastructure providers (hyperscale computing clusters).

​Why the Edge: When enterprise hardware integrators like Dell (+2.87%) take off on heavy bids, cloud cluster operators generally follow. With CRWV up only +1.16% and NBIS up +0.91%, capital is quietly stepping in at VWAP without triggering runaway breakout slippage yet.

​3. Credo Technology (CRDO) — +1.23%
​The Role: High-speed connectivity (Active Electrical Cables / AECs) essential for interconnecting AI server racks.

​The Chart Footprint: While optical component plays (CIEN, LITE) are deep in the red, CRDO is holding firm in positive territory, chopping sideways right at the highs of its opening range.
​Why the Edge: It acts as the physical plumbing between GPUs. It is showing clear relative strength against the battered optical/telecom hardware sector and hasn't staged a vertical expansion candle yet.

​4. TE Connectivity (TEL) — +0.87%
​The Role: Power connectors, sensor components, and high-voltage cabling for industrial computing and power infrastructure.

​The Chart Footprint: A classic sharp capitulation wick followed by an immediate recovery back across the baseline into green.

​Why the Edge: Highly liquid, large-cap institutional favorite that lags high-beta names on day one of a rotation before institutional block orders grind it up over multiple sessions.

​The Playbook: Catch-Up vs. Value Traps
CategoryWatchlist TickersSetup Verdict

High-Probability Catch-UpMOD, CRWV, NBISPrime. They share the exact same end-market narrative as today's leaders (cooling, compute capacity) and show clear buying wicks off session lows.

Relative Strength StandoutsCRDO, TELSolid. Holding green while their broader sub-sectors (telecom/components) are red; steady accumulation pattern.

Avoid (False Laggards)AMAT, LRCX, ASML, CIENAvoid. Down 1% to 9% on downward-sloping intraday trendlines. These are sources of funds for the rotation, not resting bid setups.

These intraday charts show a clear, textbook sub-industry rotation within the broader tech and AI ecosystem: capital is rotating out of semiconductor hardware and wafer fab equipment into physical AI infrastructure, hyperscale data owners, and select photonics/LiDAR plays.

​Where Money Is Flowing Out (The Laggards)
​Semiconductor Capital Equipment (WFE) & Testing: Pure-play equipment and testing providers are experiencing broad selling pressure (AMAT -1.04%, LRCX -0.74%, KLAC -0.89%, ASML -1.81%, TER -1.0%, ONTO -0.82%, KLIC -0.87%, AEHR -1.52%).
​Foundries & Memory/Storage: Foundry exposure (TSM -1.33%, UMC -1.38%) and storage/memory plays (MU -0.71%, WDC -1.34%, SNDK -1.55%, DRAM -1.57%) are uniformly negative, signaling a cooling off from upstream hardware fabrication.
​Optical Networking Components: Traditional optical transport/telecom networking is taking a hit, led sharply lower by CIEN (-9.36%), with downstream drag on LITE (-2.16%) and AAOI (-1.74%).

​Where Money Is Flowing In (The Leaders)
​AI Data Center Infrastructure, Power & Liquid Cooling: This is the strongest pocket of relative strength. Names tied directly to power distribution, rack architecture, and thermal management are leading across the board (VRT +3.45%, TSSI +3.58%, POWL +2.72%, DELL +2.87%, NVT +2.14%, ANET +1.78%).
​Hyperscalers & Data Owners: Mega-cap monetization and platforms are acting as relative safe havens or growth beneficiaries (META +2.72%, GOOGL +1.20%), while smaller advertising-dependent social plays (RDDT -2.04%) are lagging.
​Specialized Sensing & Industrial Photonics: Rather than standard telecom fiber, capital is favoring LiDAR and specialized optics (HSAI +12.21%, LASR +4.82%, LASE +3.04%, OUST +2.87%).
​The Takeaway
The market is de-risking upstream cyclical supply chain names (chips, memory, and fabrication tooling) that ran hot, and reallocating directly into the physical bottlenecks of AI scaling—specifically megawatts, thermal/cooling systems, and turnkey server integrators.

Looking at the charts provided (1-Month 15m, multi-day 15m, and intraday 5m), Applied Materials (AMAT) has been in a persistent, stair-stepping downtrend from its mid-August peak above $560 down to its current price near $434.

​1. Key Support & Resistance Levels
​Support Levels
​Near-Term Immediate Support: $425.00 – $427.00
​Seen on the intraday 5m chart: AMAT flushed down to ~$426 this morning on a high-volume capitulation spike before bouncing back toward VWAP. This represents today’s liquidity low and the immediate floor buyers must defend.

​Secondary / Breakdown Support: $418.00 – $420.00
​The lower boundary of the current 1-month downward channel (visible on the 1-month 15m chart). A loss of $425 invites a slide directly into the $420 round-number shelf.
​Long-Term Macro Base: $395.00 – $400.00

​The major multi-month structural shelf below the current consolidation pattern. If wafer-fab equipment (WFE) spending forecasts take another hit, $400 serves as the primary macro institutional accumulation zone.
​Resistance Levels
​Near-Term Overhead Ceiling: $438.00 – $442.00

​The descending VWAP line and upper Bollinger Band on the 5m/15m chart. Every intraday relief pop over the last two sessions has stalled out exactly at this shelf.
​Intermediate Breakdown Shelf: $456.00 – $460.00
​Prior consolidation support from late August (visible clearly on the 15m chart) that broke down violently. In technical market structure, this prior floor is now hardened overhead supply.

​Long-Term Structural Resistance: $485.00 – $510.00
​The mid-August distribution plateau. Any sustained trend reversal must reclaim $485 to confirm that the broader downtrend has ended.

​2. When to Expect a Recovery (Technical & Cyclical Timeline)
​The Intraday vs. Higher Timeframe Picture
​Intraday (Signs of Exhaustion): On the 5-minute chart, AMAT put in a sharp "V" wick off the $426 flush with heavy volume, and the MACD briefly crossed positive before stalling near $437. While selling intensity is slowing down, there is no higher-timeframe trend reversal yet—it is currently just an oversold relief bounce inside a declining channel.

​The Structural Headwind: Today’s broader board shows capital actively rotating out of WFE and testing equipment (ASML -2.33%, LRCX -0.46%, KLAC -0.47%, AMAT -0.97%) and into physical power, cooling, and server builders (DELL, VRT, ANET). As long as equipment is being used as the "source of funds," rallies in AMAT will face aggressive fade selling into resistance.

​The Recovery Roadmap
​Phase 1: Lateral Basing (Next 1–2 Weeks / Mid-September):
​AMAT needs to stop making lower lows and build an accumulation base between $425 and $445. Expect choppy, sideways action rather than an immediate V-recovery back to $500+.

​Phase 2: Reversal Confirmation (Late September to Mid-October):
​A true swing recovery will only trigger once AMAT prints a daily close above $460 on rising volume, taking out the late-August breakdown shelf and turning the declining 20-day moving average upward.

​Catalyst Window:
​Sustained institutional re-entry into chip equipment typically waits for late-quarter guidance updates or pre-earnings color (mid-to-late October) before capital rotates back into upstream fab tooling.
​Bottom Line

​Don't rush to front-run the bottom with short-dated call options while AMAT remains below $442. The lowest-risk approach is to wait for the $425–$430 base to be tested and held over multiple sessions, or wait for a confirmed volume breakout above $460 to confirm that the month-long distribution phase is officially over.

**EOD Action and who's on deck**

Money is not rotating into memory and the bottom of the board today—the green in SNDK (+0.58%) is just a low-beta drift, not institutional accumulation.

​The heat map and intraday price action show institutions doubling down on rack-scale servers, physical buildouts, and cloud hyperscalers, while actively dumping upstream WFE and punishing equipment suppliers.

​1. The Real Rotation Happening on Your Screen
​Top of the Board (Leaders): DELL (+5.12%), VRT (+4.84%), CAMT (+4.08%), CRWV (+3.67%), TSSI (+3.58%), POWL (+3.33%)
​The intraday charts on all of these show simultaneous, vertical mid-day volume spikes that broke into new highs. Capital is concentrated in rack integration (DELL, TSSI), power/switchgear (POWL), cooling (VRT), and neo-cloud compute capacity (CoreWeave).

​Look at Data Owners at the very bottom: META (+3.63%) and GOOGL (+1.68%) caught an identical mid-day institutional surge. The market is bidding the hyperscalers and the immediate tier-1 contractors building their physical clusters.

​Bottom of the Board (Laggards & Sells): WDC (-0.38%), DRAM (-0.47%), MU (-0.57%), LRCX (+0.13%), AMAT (-0.81%), KLAC (-0.69%)

​Memory (Micron, Western Digital, DRAM ETF) and Wafer Fab Equipment (LRCX, AMAT, KLAC) are in the red or flatlining near unchanged.

​The intraday patterns show a sharp mid-day flush that barely recovered to baseline VWAP. There is zero institutional urgency to buy memory or front-end tools into this afternoon's tape.

​The Warning Signal (CIEN -10.61%):
​Ciena reported fiscal Q3 earnings this morning. Even though they delivered a record quarter, the stock was dumped over 10%. The street is showing severe intolerance for optical/networking or high-multiple hardware names that don't match the immediate cash-conversion velocity of direct server rack integrators like Dell.

​2. Where Does the Capital Rotate Next When Cooling Cools Off?
​When cooling names (VRT, MOD, NVT) eventually take a multi-day breather to digest these moves, money will not blindly dive into the red memory bin. Institutional flow follows the critical path of the data center build:
​Intra-Rack High-Speed Connectivity (The Immediate Next Leg):

​Before memory chips matter, the physical racks must be connected without data bottlenecks. Keep a close eye on ALAB (Astera Labs, +2.10%) and MRVL (Marvell, +2.54%). Retimers, PCIe Gen 6 switches, and Active Electrical Cables (AECs) are the bridge between the physical racks and the silicon.
​Advanced Packaging & Metrology Over Bare WFE:

​Notice that while front-end toolmakers (AMAT, LRCX) are red, CAMT (Camtek, +4.08%) is near the very top of your board. Packaging and 3D-chiplet inspection (CoWoS/HBM verification) is the only semiconductor tool segment consistently holding a bid because it directly gates Blackwell and next-gen GPU delivery.

​When Does Memory Actually Catch the Bid?
​Memory (MU, WDC, SNDK) will get its turn, but typically as a late-cycle catch-up play. For memory to lead a rotation, you need to see High Bandwidth Memory (HBM4) contract pricing hikes confirmed or a structural bottoming in enterprise SSD pricing. Until then, institutional desks treat memory as cyclical and volatile, preferring the multi-year backlogs of physical infrastructure.

**Final EOD positions**

NVT is the next rotation I’d attack first. MOD second. TEL third. CC is the name you’re remembering—Chemours—but I would rank it behind the direct equipment suppliers.

The rotation path is:
VRT — leader; already moving.
NVT / MOD — direct liquid-cooling and thermal laggards.

TEL / POWL / ANET — internal power distribution, connections and networking.
TSSI / DELL / SMCI — rack and server integration.

CC — cooling-fluid optionality.

Why NVT first: it recently fell from roughly $177 to $148 and is only around $152–153 despite today’s bounce. It still has catch-up room toward $155–156, then $160–162. It gives us the cleanest combination of direct thematic exposure, recent compression and available upside. 


MOD is the more direct cooling play, but it has already recovered to about $184 after touching $175. Its next barriers are approximately $188–190, then $195–198. 

CC clarification: Chemours makes Opteon 2P50, a dielectric fluid for two-phase immersion cooling. That is legitimate data-center cooling exposure. However, immersion cooling is only one subset of liquid cooling, and Chemours is still moving through qualification and commercialization. Therefore, CC is an asymmetric secondary play—not as clean or immediate as NVT or MOD. 

We already own POWL and ANET, so that part of the rotation is covered. I wouldn’t add VRT, DELL, CRWV or TSSI after today’s vertical move.

My order for the saved capital: NVT → MOD → CC. Send me the NVT and MOD five-minute/hourly charts plus their 9/18 chains. My preliminary structures are NVT 155/160 and MOD 185/190 or 190/195, depending on the debit and exact resistance. The broader cooling/power bid has fundamental support, although VRT also has company-specific fuel from yesterday’s microgrid acquisition.
